# A040 / B08: quantitative insufficiency and a best-coordinate guard

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All functions below are actual Boolean signs on uniform physical cubes. Logs are base two, except ln. Write P(S)=fhat(S)^2, d_i=P(i in S), I=sum d_i, M=sum h2(d_i), B_i=H(S_i|S_-i), J_i=I(S_i;S_-i)=h2(d_i)-B_i, and TC=M-H. The campaign B is sum B_i, not M. Q is the product of the spectral marginals, A=supp P, K_supp=-log Q(A), K_shape=D(P||Q(.|A)), so TC=K_supp+K_shape.

The collision convention tested is exactly A036 v1: b_S=sign(fhat(S))*sqrt(Q(S)) on A and zero off A, C_b(U)=sum b_S b_(S xor U), v_U=C_b(U)^2, Z=sum v_U, R=v/Z, T=sum v_U log(1/v_U)/Z=H(R)-log Z. When Q(A)<1 this b is subnormalized; its support conditioning must be stated separately. No unspecified completion at spectral zeros is being tested.

We prove three controls. First, addressing tensored with a fixed three-bit point defect has strictly positive TC, K_supp, K_shape and T, each Theta(1), while H=Theta(a); every one of these credit/entropy ratios is Theta(1/a). Second, a quadratic bent function with one flipped vertex has strictly positive J_i at every coordinate, J_i/B_i~4*2^-k/ln2, and TC/H~8*2^-k/(k ln2). Thus every physical-coordinate posterior KL can be positive and asymptotically negligible even at the best coordinate. Third, the specified B05 family does NOT have this defect at the best coordinate: min_i B_i/d_i ->2 and best-coordinate J_i/h2(d_i)->1. Sparse tribes also do not falsify the normalized collision mechanism: T/(4I)=w-3+o(1), (M-T/4)/I ->2+log2 e, and (TC-T/4)/I ->log2 e-2.

These refute any universal positive-fraction extraction of entropy by the specified credits alone. They do not refute payment with additional ordinary influence, FEI, the full-support collision-admission premise, or arbitrary references/completions.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

Read EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.json in full (fei-execution-protocol/2, 2026-10-11-execution-v2), campaign README and CURRENT_STATUS, the four registries/next-action files and the .tex plan. BOARD.md was read at stage start (snapshot 2026-10-11 22:57 UTC). B08 STREAM_NOTES.md v1, 2026-10-11 22:45Z supplied the live target and notation guard. Read A036 runs/20261011T214139Z-account05-A036-reference-law-divergence/REPORT.md, especially Sections 1,2,7; the exact signed support convention comes from that source. Read the earlier canonical B05 run 20261011T193057Z-account01-B05-rare-restrictions/REPORT.md, Sections 2-7, and the supplementary CAMPAIGN, CLAIMS, COUNTEREXAMPLES, SEED_LEMMAS, NATIVE_MAPPING. Source sections consumed: ERASURE_REDUCTION Sections 1-5; BLOCK_RESTRICTION_IDENTITY Sections 1-3; ROUTE_MAP_CURATED Sections 2,4,9.1; GAMMA_ENDPOINT_LEDGER was inspected for separation of occurrence credits from the present reference.

The four provided paper texts were loaded and searched; relevant entropy/reference/restriction passages were inspected. Threshold 38-page and earlier 29-page versions: reference normalization, composition and Theorem 6.6. Percolation 30-page and earlier 31-page versions: Section 11.2, Corollary 11.3, support-sensitive divergence. They are context, not proof premises; no whole-paper audit is claimed. These texts use a legacy cp1252-compatible encoding; an initial utf-8 read failed and was corrected without changing them. Local source hashes and locators are saved in out/source_receipts.json. No brain query or web source was needed for the self-contained derivations.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Addressing: keep the entire support loss

Let m=2^a and F_a(x,y)=y_x, with a address bits and m fresh data bits. Its coefficients are m^-1*(-1)^(V dot j) at (V,{j}), for every V,j, and zero otherwise. Consequently P is uniform on m^2 atoms, H=2a, d_address=1/2, d_data=1/m, I=1+a/2. Every support atom has product-reference mass Q(S)=w_a/m^2, where w_a=(1-1/m)^(m-1). Therefore Q(A)=w_a, Q(.|A)=P, TC=K_supp=-log w_a, K_shape=0, and b=sqrt(w_a)*fhat.

Booleanity gives sum fhat(S)fhat(S xor U)=1[U=0], so C_b(U)=w_a*1[U=0], Z=w_a^2, H(R)=0 and T=-2log w_a. This explicitly keeps the -log Z term: discarding it would incorrectly declare T=0 under the subnormalized convention. If instead b is conditioned by dividing by sqrt(w_a), Z=1 and T=0 exactly. A completion outside A changes the problem; no completed-reference conclusion follows here.

Let h be the three-bit sign equal to -1 at one vertex and +1 elsewhere. Its spectrum is (9/16,1/16,...,1/16), its marginals are 1/4, H_h=4-(9/16)log 9, I_h=3/4, and K_h=3h2(1/4)-H_h>0. The strict KL sign follows because this full-support spectrum is not product. A036's direct formula, also rechecked here, gives v_0=1, v_1=3/256 per singleton, v_2=9/256 per pair, v_3=27/256, Z_h=319/256 and T_h>0. All nonzero v_U<1 except v_0, so T_h is strictly positive without a numerical sign assumption.

For G_a=F_a*h on disjoint variables, actual phases and b tensorize. Hence

    H(G_a)=2a+H_h, I(G_a)=a/2+7/4,
    K_supp=-log w_a, K_shape=K_h, TC=-log w_a+K_h,
    Z=w_a^2*(319/256), T=-2log w_a+T_h.

Because -log w_a ->log2 e, the four strictly positive credit ratios to H satisfy

    a*K_supp/H ->(log2 e)/2,
    a*K_shape/H ->K_h/2,
    a*TC/H ->(log2 e+K_h)/2,
    a*T/H ->(2log2 e+T_h)/2.

Likewise T/(M-T/4)=Theta(1/a), since M=H+TC=2a+O(1). This supplies positive, quantitative credit-insufficiency witnesses with both support and normalization retained. However H/I ->4; finite-price influence can pay the growing residual. In particular this example is NOT a refutation of M<=T/4+cI for sufficiently large c.

### 2. A best-coordinate obstruction with every posterior KL positive

For even k>=4 let b_k(x)=(-1)^(x1*x2+...+x_(k-1)*x_k) in 0/1 coordinates, delta=2^-k and s=sqrt(delta). Flip its value at x=0: g_k=b_k-2*1[x=0]. The two-bit Walsh transform is (1,1,1,-1)/2; tensorizing shows c_T=s*sigma_T, sigma_T in {+1,-1}, with sigma_T=(-1)^(T1*T2+...). Point surgery subtracts 2delta from every coefficient. Thus

    P_T=delta*(sigma_T-2s)^2,
    count(sigma=+)=(2^k+2^(k/2))/2,
    count(sigma=-)=(2^k-2^(k/2))/2.

This is full support for k>=4. Flipping the central value toggles exactly one edge in each direction, giving d_i=1/2+2delta. Pair permutations and swaps within a pair act transitively on all physical coordinates and fix x=0; all B_i and J_i are equal. With the partner bit absent, sigma is unchanged on toggling i, hence the conditional spectral bit is exactly fair. With the partner present the signs are opposite. The latter pairs have total probability (1+4delta)/2 and conditional probability

    q=(1-2s)^2/[2(1+4delta)]=1/2-2s/(1+4delta).

The same-sign pairs have total probability (1-4delta)/2. Therefore the exact formula is

    B_i=(1-4delta)/2 +(1+4delta)/2*h2(q),
    J_i=h2(1/2+2delta)-B_i.

J_i>0 follows directly: same-sign pairs have positive mass and conditional probability 1/2, different from the unconditional d_i. Zero mutual information would require equality on every positive-mass packet. This proves strict positivity at every coordinate, not just nonnegativity.

The Taylor expansion h2(1/2+t)=1-2t^2/ln2+O(t^4), with its bounded fourth derivative on |t|<=1/3, gives

    B_i=1-4delta/ln2+O(delta^2),
    J_i=4delta/ln2+O(delta^2),
    J_i/B_i~4*2^-k/ln2 ->0,
    B_i/d_i ->2.

In particular every minimizer of B_i/d_i has the same vanishing credit fraction. To check global TC, write P_T=2^-k*(1+u_T), u_T=-4s*sigma_T+4delta. Under uniform spectral indices E u=0, E u^2=16delta(1-delta). For sufficiently large k, |u|<=1/2, and (1+u)ln(1+u)=u+u^2/2+O(|u|^3) with uniform remainder. It follows that D(P||Unif)=8delta/ln2+O(delta^(3/2)). Also D(Q||Unif)=k*(1-h2(d_i))=O(kdelta^2). Hence TC=8delta/ln2+o(delta), H=k-8delta/ln2+o(delta), and TC/H~8*2^-k/(k ln2). TC=K_shape>0; K_supp=0 exactly because support is full, and is not described as a positive credit.

This controls the erasure residual itself, rather than a merely large unrelated marginal entropy. It refutes J_i>=c*B_i at some coordinate for a fixed c>0, even allowing best-coordinate selection. It does not refute B_i<=K*d_i, which holds asymptotically here with K just above 2. Posterior extraction and influence payment are different tests.

The normalized collision term is also positive and insufficient on this SAME full-support family. Since s>2delta, surgery preserves every Fourier sign; these signs are the tensor products of the two-bit phase (+,+,+,-). With d=1/2+2delta the signed reference on each pair is ((1-d),sqrt(d(1-d)),sqrt(d(1-d)),-d). Its autocorrelations are C_0=1, C_10=C_01=2(1-2d)sqrt(d(1-d)), C_11=0. Put v=4(1-2d)^2*d*(1-d)=16delta^2-256delta^4. Then 0<v<1, Z=(1+2v)^(k/2), and

    T=k*v*log2(1/v)/(1+2v) ~32*k^2*delta^2,
    T/(sum_i B_i) ~32*k*delta^2 ->0.

Thus all k posterior KLs, TC=K_shape, and normalized T are strictly positive yet negligible compared with their specified unpaid erasure/entropy bills. K_supp is correctly zero because there is no support loss; the addressing tensor supplies the separate witness with strictly positive support loss as well. This does not falsify a collision lower-payment inequality TC>=T/4-kappa I: here TC is much larger than T and that lower payment is easy. The missing recovery of entropy is the relevant insufficiency.

### 3. Why B05 is not the best-coordinate insufficiency witness

Use the specified B05 construction with even k, r=2k, p=2^-2k, delta=2^-k, s=sqrt(delta), selector disagreement d=pdelta and A=1-s+delta. The exact parent Fourier coefficients, indexed by data T, control U and selector Y, are

    Y=0: 1[T=U=empty]+p*(c_T-1[T=empty]-delta),
    Y=1: d at every (T,U).

They come directly from 1+A(z)*(b(x)-1)-2*y*A(z)*1[x=0], with y=(1-chi_selector)/2. The coordinate influences are

    data: p/2+d; control: p*A; selector: d.

B05's explicit selector packets give B_selector=d*(k+log2 e+o(1)); expanding h2(d)=d*(3k+log2 e+o(1)) yields

    J_selector/h2(d) ->2/3, J_selector/B_selector ->2.

For a control coordinate, all Y=0 pairs have equal masses except the pair consisting of the all-empty coefficient and a control singleton. Write E(u,v)=(u+v)h2(v/(u+v)). The exact formula is

    B_control=2p*A-2p^2*A^2 + E((1-p*A)^2,p^2*A^2).

The Y=1 contribution d cancels the -d in the nonexceptional Y=0 mass. The exceptional term is O(kp^2). Thus B_control=2p*(1+o(1)).

For a data coordinate, restrict to Y=0 pairs with nonempty residual data index. Both coefficients have magnitude p*s*(1+O(s)); their pair entropy is their pair mass times (1+O(delta)). Their total mass is p*(1+O(s)). The omitted pairs have residual data index empty. For U nonempty their large/small masses are p^2*A^2 and p^2*(s-delta)^2; summed over fewer than 1/p controls their entropy is O(kpdelta). For U empty, their entropy is O(kp^2delta). These bounds follow from E(u,v)<=v*log2((u+v)/v)+v/ln2 when 0<v<=u. The Y=1 contribution is d. Consequently B_data=p*(1+o(1)). All bounds are uniform over the coordinates in their class.

Therefore B_data/d_data ->2, B_control/d_control ->2, whereas B_selector/d_selector~k. For all sufficiently large k a best coordinate is data or control, and

    min_i B_i/d_i ->2,
    J_best/h2(d_best) ->1.

This is the requested best-coordinate test with the opposite outcome from insufficiency. The exact k=2,4 computations are consistency checks, not a claim about all-k minimizer classification at finite k.

### 4. Sparse tribes: normalized credit remains large enough

Let there be t=w disjoint blocks of width w, p=2^-w, b=(1-p)^t and f=2*prod_j(1-1[block_j=0])-1. For w>=3, b>1/2. A nonempty Fourier set hitting j blocks has coefficient 2*(1-p)^(t-j)*(-p)^j. The empty coefficient is 2b-1>0, so the sign pattern is exactly the product of the point-defect block phases. All coefficients are nonzero; Q(A)=1, K_supp=0. Every marginal is d=2p*(1-p)^(t-1), I=t*w*d, M=t*w*h2(d).

The same signed-reference block calculation as A036, now with this d rather than d=2^(1-w), gives for a difference set of size j within one block

    v_0=1,
    v_j=[4d(1-d)]^j*[1-2^(2-j)*(1-d)^(w-j)]^2,
    Z_block=sum_j binom(w,j)*v_j,
    T=t*sum_j binom(w,j)*v_j*log2(1/v_j)/Z_block.

This keeps Z exactly and uses actual product-compatible phases. Since w^2*d ->0, the singleton mass is v_1=4d*(1+O(wd)), Z_block=1+O(wd), and its raw entropy is 4*w*d*(log2(1/d)-2+o(1)). The j>=2 terms have total entropy O(w^3*d^2): for large w, j=2 has the extra vanishing bracket, while j>=3 has bracket bounded away from zero and at most one; summing binom(w,j)*(4d)^j*j*O(w) gives the asserted upper bound. Dividing by w*d makes that remainder o(1). Thus

    T/(4I)=log2(1/d)-2+o(1)=w-3+o(1),
    M/I=log2(1/d)+log2 e+o(1),
    (M-T/4)/I ->2+log2 e.

The exact entropy formula, with v=4b(1-b) and e=(2b-1)^2, is

    H=e*log2(1/e)-v*log2(4b^2)+8*b*t*p*log2((1-p)/p).

Here tp->0, so H/I->4 and (TC-T/4)/I ->log2 e-2. Thus TC is asymptotically T/4 up to an ordinary influence-sized adjustment. This family does not furnish tiny collision credit; it supplies a surviving calibration. No conclusion about balanced/dense tribes is inferred.

### 5. First unsupported universal line

None of these calculations bounds sum B_i for arbitrary Boolean f by O(I), or constructs a nonreusing tree payment after reference resets. The first unsupported universal step would be to infer that the tiny-credit bent example cannot be paid by influence, or that sparse-tribes calibration implies a universal collision theorem. Both inferences are unjustified. Conversely one cannot infer small posterior credit at the best B05 coordinate from its bad selector split.

When the main checker's reduced argument x satisfies 0<=x-1<2^-80, it uses the exact elementary enclosure u-u^2/2<=ln(1+u)<=u with u=x-1 instead; this preserves relative accuracy without long rational series. # A040 stage 2: odd-face debit versus posterior credit on one overlapping-control tree

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Bits throughout. For a Boolean sign f on a uniform physical cube let P(S)=fhat(S)^2, d_i=Inf_i, H=-sum P log2 P, I=sum d_i, B_i=H(S_i|S_-i), J_i=h2(d_i)-B_i, TC=sum h2(d_i)-H. J_i is the spectral-coordinate posterior mutual information; it is NOT the physical-child JS information, an occupancy KL, or a leaf-reference KL. At a physical split with children f0,f1 the odd-face debit is Q_i=I(f0*f1)/2. The strict K=4 bad set is B_i>4d_i; equality is good.

This stage consumes A098's source-bound reallocation: audit localized odd-face versus posterior credit on a prescribed overlapping-control tree, retaining terminal CE. B08 v2's minimum-ratio census/calibration direction is stale in that scope: A071/A082 have completed the five-bit census, and A001/A040/A089 already solved B05 best-coordinate calibration. No fresh random census or duplicate B05 calculation was run. This departure is recorded in STATUS.md. A098's dispatch delta is a saved mathematical direction, not evidence that a schedule or shared index was changed.

The fixed overlap mask is C(z)=1[z0=0]*(1[z1=0] OR 1[z2=0]). It represents two events sharing their physical common control z0 and the same data core. Put alpha=EC=3/8, L=I(C)/2=5/8. For even k>=4 use b_k(x)=(-1)^(x1*x2+...+x_(k-1)*x_k), b'_k=b_k-2*1[x=0], and define

    F_k(x,z,y)=1+C(z)*(b_k(x)-1-2*y*1[x=0]).

The adaptive tree queries selector y first, then the common control, then z1, and z2 only if z1 fails. It stops at a constant or at b_k/b'_k; no data coordinate is queried and no irrelevant resolved control is queried. Let delta=2^-k and s=sqrt(delta). We prove:

    B_root=alpha*delta*(k+log2 e+o(1)),
    J_root=alpha*delta*log2(8/3)+o(delta)>0,
    Q_root=alpha*delta*(k+5/3),
    J_root/B_root~log2(8/3)/k ->0,
    Q_root/J_root~k/log2(8/3) ->infinity.

All control descendants are strictly good at K=4. The selector is bad for every even k>=4, so Q_bad=Q_root. Moreover

    Q_bad/I(F_k)<=17/176, with equality at k=4,
    Q_bad/I(F_k)~2delta ->0.

Native terminal references are uniform on the k data spectral bits and deterministic on any irrelevant unqueried controls. They give CE_leaf=alpha*k, retained exactly, with CE_leaf<=2 I_leaf. These are explicitly native leaf references; this stage does not identify their average CE with a cross entropy against an unspecified root reference.

Independent review target: A023-OVERLAP-EXPOSURE-GOOD-CONTROL-LEDGER@1, accepted at its stated scope by the reconstruction below. The new root-credit comparison completes A040's existing insufficiency packet under the review-cap guard; it is a scoped test, not a new broad universal payment candidate.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Independent reconstruction of A023's all-width control result

Take any independent Boolean core g, a=1-Eg>=11/16, compatible common point controls of width s0>=0, and two exclusive point blocks of widths r,t>=1. Write u=2^-s0, p=2^-r, q=2^-t, beta=p+q-pq. The physical event union is A_s0*(A_r OR A_t). A common-control edge is pivotal exactly when its other common controls match, the union occurs, and g=-1. An exclusive edge also needs the other exclusive event to fail. Direct edge counting therefore gives

    I_ctrl=u*a*[s0*beta+r*p*(1-q)+t*q*(1-p)],
    I_leaf=u*beta*I(g), H_leaf=u*beta*H(g).

The terminal probability is u*beta, not u*(p+q); the intersection and the shared core are counted once. All paths query each physical control at most once.

At a first-exclusive split with l controls remaining, set rho=2^-(l-1). With C0 the independent competing event, the two children are

    f0=1+C0*(g-1),
    f1=1+[C0+(1-C0)*A_(l-1)]*(g-1),
    d=a*rho*(1-q)/2.

In every Fourier packet with a nonempty label among the l-1 remaining fresh controls, f0 has coefficient zero. The parent pair then has equal squared masses, so its erasure is twice its mismatch mass. The point indicator has empty-label squared-mass fraction rho. Exactly 2(1-rho)d of erasure is thus already accounted for; the remaining packets have total mismatch rho*d and total packet mass at most one. Concavity of binary entropy and monotonicity of m*h2(e/m) in m give

    B/d<=2(1-rho)+rho*log2(e/(rho*d)).

If a(1-q)>e/8, this is strictly less than 2+2rho*(1+log2(1/rho))<=4, since rho=2^-j and (j+1)2^-j<=1 for every integer j>=0. Here q<=1/2 and a>=11/16, hence a(1-q)>=11/32>e/8. The condition depends on the actual node, not an assumed unsigned mismatch channel.

For a constant-child split, independently write its nonconstant child mean as 1-2d. The nonempty residual Fourier packets have equal parent pair masses, while the empty packet masses are (1-d)^2,d^2. Thus, with E(v,w)=(v+w)h2(w/(v+w)),

    B=2d(1-d)+E((1-d)^2,d^2).

Define the signed scalar A(d)=d-d^2+d^2 log2(((1-d)^2+d^2)/d^2), continuously at endpoints. The difference B-A is d-d^2+(1-d)^2 log2(1+d^2/(1-d)^2), at most d-d^2+(log2 e)d^2<=d*log2 e. Since the packet sum is at most one and d log2(1/d)<=1/(e ln2),

    B/d<=1+log2 e+2/(e ln2)<4.

This includes negative child means and d=1; d=0 has zero bill. This derivation uses the actual parent spectrum and does not identify signed mismatch with the magnitude minority. For genuine magnitude-minority debt lambda, lambda<=B follows directly by dropping the positive companion entropy term, so A023's stated lambda bounds also follow.

Exact analytic constants are ln2>56/81 and 65/24<e<65/24+1/100<11/4. They follow respectively from two positive atanh terms and the exponential series through degree four with the remainder bounded by a geometric tail from 1/5!. These imply the strict threshold and the stated constants 4+2/ln2<7 and 4+3/ln2<9. With JS<=2d/ln2 and the exact entropy/influence telescopes, all A023 preterminal cost bounds follow. For its fair bent-surgery terminal class, H(g)<=k+1 and I(g)>=k/2-(k-1)delta>=7k/16, so H(g)/I(g)<=20/7<3; its mean gives a>=1-s-delta>=11/16. This reconstructs the full stated scoped class result without repairing it. Verdict: HOLDS_AT_STATED_SCOPE, locally independent acceptance. The source contributor remains A023; A040 is a reviewer, not a repairing contributor.

### 2. Exact overlap selector packets and the positive but insufficient KL

The two-bit bent Walsh spectrum is (+,+,+,-)/2. Tensoring gives b_hat(T)=s*sigma_T and sum_T sigma_T=2^(k/2). At the selector split the two actual child coefficients are those of 1+C(b-1) and 1+C(b'-1). Their difference is 2delta*C_hat(U) for every data label T. For T nonempty the midpoint is (s*sigma_T-delta)*C_hat(U). Thus

    e_(T,U)=delta^2*C_hat(U)^2,
    q_(T,U)=C_hat(U)^2*((s*sigma_T-delta)^2+delta^2).

Summing over U multiplies the nonempty-data erasure by sum C_hat(U)^2=alpha. There are (2^k+2^(k/2))/2-1 positive nonempty data signs and (2^k-2^(k/2))/2 negative ones. For empty data, nonempty controls contribute with total squared-mask mass alpha-alpha^2, midpoint factor s-1-delta, and mismatch delta^2; the all-empty packet has midpoint 1+alpha*(s-1-delta) and mismatch alpha^2 delta^2. These four packet classes are explicit, normalized and used by the grouped exact checker.

In the nonempty-data packets theta=e/q=delta*(1+O(s)) uniformly. Their mismatch total is alpha*delta*(1-delta). Applying h2(theta)=theta*(log2(1/theta)+log2 e+O(theta)) gives their erasure alpha*delta*(k+log2 e+o(1)). The empty-data control packets have mismatch O(delta^2), so their entropy is O(kdelta^2)=o(delta); the all-empty packet obeys the same bound. Consequently

    B_root=alpha*delta*(k+log2 e+o(1)).

Since d=alpha*delta, h2(d)=alpha*delta*(k+log2(1/alpha)+log2 e+o(1)), proving J_root=alpha*delta*log2(8/3)+o(delta). Strict positivity for every k>=4 also follows without asymptotics: both nonempty-data sign classes have positive mass, and their conditional selector probabilities delta^2/((s-delta)^2+delta^2) and delta^2/((s+delta)^2+delta^2) differ. A zero posterior MI would require equality on every positive packet.

For k>=8, q/e>=1/(4delta) on all nonempty-data packets gives B_root/d>=(1-delta)(k-2)>4. The finite k=4,6 rational sign certificates close the remaining two even endpoints. Thus the selector root is bad for all even k>=4. Positivity alone pays no fixed fraction of its bill: J_root/B_root~log2(8/3)/k.

### 3. Root face ownership, exact budget and retained terminal CE

The root child product is 1-2*C*1[x=0]. Its data edge contribution to influence is 2alpha*kdelta and its control contribution is delta*I(C). Direct enumeration of the eight control assignments gives EC=3/8 and I(C)=5/4. Therefore

    Q_root=I(child product)/2=alpha*delta*(k+5/3).

After the selector is revealed, each terminal core is b or b'. Their deficits are 1-s and 1-s+2delta, both at least 3/4 for k>=4. The independent A023 reconstruction applies; every control descendant is good. The only bad debit is therefore Q_root. This debit is a set of actual odd faces charged at their first revealed direction, not a posterior KL and not a second claim on a control influence account.

The one-flip physical edge count gives I(b)=k/2, I(b')=k/2+2kdelta, and selector-core influence delta. Setting a=1-s+delta yields the exact resources

    I(F_k)=alpha*(k/2+(k+1)delta)+a*L,
    I_removed=alpha*delta+a*L,
    I_leaf=alpha*(k/2+kdelta),
    I(F_k)=I_removed+I_leaf.

These are the same physical tree's accounts. In particular the common control is not assigned once to each event; L=5/8 is the actual union's single account. Since I(F_k)>=alpha*k/2,

    Q_bad/I(F_k)<=2delta*(1+5/(3k)).

For k>=6 this is at most 23/576<17/176. At k=4, the exact values are I=11/8, Q_bad=17/128, hence ratio 17/176. This proves the stated all-k maximum; as k->infinity I(F_k)~alpha*k/2, so Q_bad/I(F_k)~2delta. Q_bad/J_root~k/log2(8/3) diverges despite this vanishing global price. This is insufficiency of the named KL credit, not a disproof of FEI or of ordinary influence payment.

At every active leaf choose Q_leaf uniform on the data subsets and put probability one on absence of any irrelevant control labels. It is a normalized reference on the leaf's full positive support. CE_leaf_node=k for both b and b'; constants use their deterministic exact reference. The total active leaf probability is alpha, so

    CE_terminal=alpha*k,
    H_terminal=alpha*(H(b)+H(b'))/2,
    KL_terminal=CE_terminal-H_terminal>=0,
    CE_terminal<=2 I_leaf.

For example k=4 retains CE_terminal=3/2 and I_leaf=27/32. No terminal entropy is deleted because the activation is rare. The root payment can be verified directly without importing an isoperimetric premise: B_root<=h2(d)<=d*(k+log2(8/3)+log2 e)<=Q_root+(log2 e)d, since log2(8/3)<5/3, equivalently (8/3)^3<2^5. Controls satisfy B<4d by the independent exposure proof. The exact physical entropy telescope then gives the complete native-leaf certificate

    H(F_k)<= (4+2/ln2)*I_removed+Q_bad+CE_terminal
           <= (4+2/ln2+17/176)*I(F_k)<7I(F_k).

The scalar last sign follows from 1/ln2<81/56. The useful delta is the explicit localized face/credit distinction and single-use stopping ledger, not a novelty claim for FEI on this easy terminal class.

### 4. Compensated posterior ledger and the first unsupported line

At each actual split, define Gamma_i=sum_(j!=i)[h2(d_j(parent))-(h2(d_j(child0))+h2(d_j(child1)))/2]. Marginal influence averages exactly for j!=i. Combining TC=M-H, H-parent-minus-average-H=B+JS, and h2(d_i)-B=J_i gives

The universal scalar JS allowance used above is independently justified as follows. Put D(z)=((1+z)ln(1+z)+(1-z)ln(1-z))/2. On [0,1], D(z)<=1-sqrt(1-z^2), because atanh(z)<=z/sqrt(1-z^2), with the latter comparison following by differentiating their difference. For a parent amplitude pair u,v the packet JS is (u^2+v^2)D(2uv/(u^2+v^2))/ln2, so it is at most 2min(u^2,v^2)/ln2. Summation gives JS<=2d_i/ln2. Endpoints follow by continuity. The exact compensated identity is

    TC(parent)=J_i-JS_i+Gamma_i+average TC(child).

This is a new-family use of A039's identity, not a repeated universal census. The checker retains and compares exact prime-log vectors for

    sum w J_i + sum w Gamma_i + E TC_leaf = TC_root + sum w JS_i,
    sum w B_i + sum w JS_i + E H_leaf = H_root,
    sum w d_i + E I_leaf = I_root,
    sum w Q_i + E Q_leaf = Q_root_function.

Here Q_root_function is the full root odd-face potential, distinct from the selector node debit Q_root. Native terminal reference KL is CE-H, while terminal TC uses the product of its marginals; they are NOT identical on b'. Neither is pooled with the root posterior. No conclusion about the signed Bayes/occupancy reset portions of the entire A039 claim is inferred merely from these equalities.

First unsupported universal line: arbitrary overlapping controls with arbitrary cores admit a history-measurable stopping order whose K=4 localized face debit is O(I), whose required source-compatible reference reset is paid once, and whose actual terminal CE is uniformly certified. This stage proves that statement only for the declared two-event mask and the explicitly certified bent-surgery terminals. It neither builds a universal compatible root witness from the native leaf laws nor pays arbitrary terminal cores. A failure of the sufficient exposure criterion a(1-q)>e/8 is not itself a bad-node certificate.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

The complete independent derivation is REVIEW_A023.md. Let beta=q+r-qr, eta=pqr, pi=(beta+eta)/2, e=beta-eta for p=2^-L,q=2^-R,r=2^-T. The controller children are A0=WUV and A1=U OR V. They are nested; the boundary edge sets are disjoint coordinate by coordinate. Thus J(D)=J(A0)+J(A1)=2Jrem for D=A1-A0 and J(E)=I(1-2E)/2. With a balanced core,

    d_X=e/2,  I(F)=d_X+Jrem+pi I(g),
    c_X=I(F0F1)/2=Jrem+(e/2)I(g).

Every descendant is strictly 4-good by independently reconstructed constant-child and fresh two-point exposure bounds. Therefore Q_bad is zero or c_X, giving Q_bad<=I(F)-d_X. The root consumes exactly the one-unit remaining-controller reserve and fraction e/(2pi) of the terminal-core unit reserve. Its unused terminal capacity is eta I(g), not a fresh reserve at descendants.

The entropy and complete minority-KL accounts, retaining the terminal, satisfy

    H(F)<=(4+2/ln2)d_X+(5+2/ln2)Jrem+pi H(g)+(e/2)I(g),
    J_tree+pi H(g)<=5(d_X+Jrem)+pi H(g)+(e/2)I(g).

The separate 3mu/ln2 allowance costs at most (3/ln2)(d_X+Jrem). Consequently H(g)<=C0 I(g) implies H(F)<=max(5+2/ln2,C0+1)I(F). The source balanced bent-surgery terminal has H/I<3 uniformly in even k>=4, so all its claimed 8,5,10 constants follow. These are source conclusions accepted without altering their hypotheses.

**Quantitative credit insufficiency in the same retained-terminal interface.** Fix L=R=T=1 and g_k=z*b_k, k even>=2. Then pi=7/16, eta=1/8, H(g_k)=k, I(g_k)=1+k/2. Every control posterior and minority-KL term is constant under bent spectral replication. At the root, posterior J_X, complete D2(nu||Q), and its weighted credit mu D2(nu||Q) are all strictly positive; mu=1/4. Their exact prime-log vectors and positive rational enclosures are in out/CREDIT_FIXTURE.json. Each named credit C obeys the exact ratio

    C/[pi H(g_k)]=(16 C/7)/k = Theta(1/k).

Also H(F_k)=H(F_dictator)+(7/16)k and I(F_k)=3/4+(7/16)(1+k/2), so H/I tends to 2. This closes the required asymptotic test for the named positive credits without mistaking it for a FEI disproof or asserting that total correlation is small.

First unsupported universal line: the sum of marked reserves at arbitrary overlapping separator ancestors is bounded per actual physical coordinate, and all arbitrary terminal entropy is paid. The theorem audited here spends its controller reserve once; it proves no renewal rule.

The complete reconstruction is REVIEW_A046.md. For a j-bit point switch between arbitrary actual Boolean branches of disagreement gamma, the empty residual-selector Fourier row plus the nonempty rows gives

    B/d<=2+2^-k[log2(euler)-2+log2(1/gamma)+2k], k=j-1.

In this controller gamma=e/2>=2^{-(min(R,T)+1)}. Elementary dyadic bounds prove every j>m layer is strictly 4-good. For alpha=2^-s, eta=2^{-(L+R+T)}, e=q+r-qr-eta, JB=(L+R+T)eta, JC=R q(1-r)+T r(1-q), JD=JB+JC and pi=eta+alpha e, direct disjoint physical cuts yield

    I_sep=s alpha e,
    I_other=(1-alpha)JB+alpha JC,
    I_terminal=pi I(g), terminal entropy=pi H(g),
    c_j=2^-j[JD+(j-1)e+e I(g)].

Independently define V_j as the expected sum of future eligible faces, sum_{ell<=min(j,m)}2^{-(j-ell)}c_ell, rather than assuming the source formula. Evaluating that sum recovers its exact V_j. Its expected decrement is c_j at j<=m and zero earlier. No reserve is reopened. Thus Q_bad<=V_s<=M I and

The terminal-core hypothesis is retained. The source's additional root-spectral/output conversion discussion is outside the exact reviewed claim and is not independently accepted here.

# A041: biased weighted pivotal intervals and a necessary endpoint tax

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

The packet target is an arity-independent replacement probability or entropy estimate for actual Boolean weighted thresholds under arbitrary product biases, surviving the three recovered physical-input TC/pivotal normalization controls. The stronger campaign target remains corrected FEI+: `H_mu^+(f) <= C [I_mu(f)-Var_mu(f)]` for all real weights, all arities, and all product biases. **That stronger target and full uniform FEI remain OPEN.**

Two source-complete auxiliary results are proved here, pending independent review:

1. For the i-th pivotal interval and every integer r>=1, coordinates of weight at least w_i/r have fiber height at most r. With `L=2 sum min(q_j,1-q_j)` on those coordinates and derivative spectral rank K, `E_nu (1-epsilon)^K <= min(1,r/sqrt(epsilon L))`. The positive logarithmic rank score `ln^+[L/(r^2(K+1))]` has mean at most `3+ln 2`. A simultaneous choice among all dyadic scales, using prior `pi_k=1/[(k+1)(k+2)]` and score `sup_k pi_k^2 L_k/[4^k(K_k+1)]`, retains that same constant. Summing the score against the actual coordinate Fourier masses costs at most `(3+ln 2) I` in **nats**. The proof covers arbitrary positive real weights; orientation covers signed weights, and contraction covers zero weights/deterministic biases.
2. For actual Boolean `f=1-2XY`, `X~Ber(p)`, `Y~Ber(1/4)`, revealing X, the difference between physical-child mixture information J and conditional spectral-label entropy M satisfies `lim_{p->0}(J-M)/[p log_2(1/p)]=1/2`. Thus a scalar tax valid for every Boolean one-bit reveal must have asymptotic lower coefficient at least 1/2; no finite `Kp` tax works. This result is in **bits**.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

`WEIGHTED_THRESHOLD_REPLACEMENT.tex` contains the full proof. A pivotal interval has width w_i and is half-open. On the selected coordinates every chain with r strict steps increases the score by at least w_i, so its support has height at most r and partitions into r antichains. Independent biased refresh has an independent fair-activation count of mean epsilon L. Conditional Sperner and `E[1/(F+1)] <= 1/(epsilon L)` give the relative noise bound. The orthonormal noise identity turns it into a derivative spectral bound. Taking epsilon=1/(2b), where b is the rank cutoff, gives the tail `sqrt(2e) exp(-u/2)`. At dyadic scale k the prior-weighted tail has factor pi_k; the factors sum exactly to one. Integration gives `3+ln 2`, without an arity factor or an uncharged choice of scale.

The theorem does not estimate conditional-input TC. For the recovered weighted family, the entire fair-activation mass is below 2, so every prior-weighted rank score is below 1/2 and all its positive logarithms vanish. In the fixed-m, q->0 local-TC regime the same conclusion holds eventually. Both controls survive directly. The pivotal-credit failure remains exactly **coefficient one**, with no inference about larger multipliers.

The elementary singleton corrected entropy is bounded by `4 Delta` in nats for every monotone gate; the proof is recorded in the TeX. The **first unsupported line** in the FEI+ continuation is a bound `sum_{|S|>=2} P(S) ln(v_S/P(S)) <= K Delta`, derived from a normalized spectral reference or another structural argument. The proven rank bound costs I; it cannot be substituted for a deficit payment near dictators. Paying that higher-degree entropy would prove weighted FEI+ with constant `(K+4)/ln 2` in bits. Exact disjoint composition would then extend the gate theorem to read-once formulas without a depth factor. This implication is conditional.

`ENDPOINT_TAX_LOWER_BOUND.tex` proves the second result directly. The four Fourier masses are `((1-2pq)^2,4p(1-p)q^2,p^2v,p(1-p)v)`, v=4q(1-q). Hence `J-M=2h_2(pv)-H(P)-p h_2(v)`. Expanding the two order-p spectral atoms yields coefficient `4q(1-2q)` on `p log_2(1/p)`, equal to 1/2 at q=1/4. The old available upper tax has coefficient one; its optimal coefficient is not determined. The lower-bound family establishes necessity through an actual signed difference.

The displayed corrected-entropy constant is **below 3000**, certified by four rational terms of the ln 2 series and e>8/3; it is deliberately unoptimized. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

The complete analytic proof is `SLICE_DEFICIT_PAYMENT.tex`. Its key cancellation is not the earlier rank estimate. Distinct count thresholds have disjoint pivotal boundary events for every coordinate, at arbitrary product biases. Hence their cross Dirichlet form is zero. The covariance of nested tail signs is the negative of their high-degree deficit inner product, yielding `Cov(f0,f1) <= sqrt(Delta0 Delta1)`. For the band sign `g=f1-f0-1`, this gives `Delta(g)=Delta0+Delta1+2Cov <= 2(Delta0+Delta1)`.

Use the scalar coherent entropy inequality only on degree at least two, so its additive squared-amplitude overhead is paid by the higher-degree mass, which is at most the deficit. The singleton bound `4 Delta` in nats is also proved here for every Boolean sign, including the nonmonotone band; it does not require the gate to be monotone. The resulting two-tail entropy estimate is `E(g) <= (2c+10)(Delta0+Delta1)`.

For one exceptional input with bias p, the restrictions f0,f1 are precisely symmetric count thresholds. Coefficients avoiding that input are the actual coherent average `(1-p) f0hat+p f1hat`; coefficients containing it are `sqrt(v) dhat`, where `d=(f1-f0)/2`, rho=E d, v=4p(1-p). The exact deficit ledger is `Delta(F)=D+delta`, `D=(1-p)Delta0+pDelta1`, `delta=v rho(1-rho)`. The high-degree avoiding-input sector costs at most `(2c+2+4/e)D`; the containing-input sector costs at most `(2c+10)D+(ln 4)delta`. This proves the displayed theorem. No incoherent-mixture substitution or physical TC estimate is used. Identical or constant restrictions and deterministic endpoints are handled directly.

For arbitrary A, merge identical symmetric B slices into cells c_s(A), with probabilities pi_s. The matrix with diagonal `Delta(f_s)` and off-diagonal `-Cov(f_s,f_u)` is an exact positive-semidefinite deficit Gram matrix. It gives `2 sum_{s<u} pi_s pi_u Cov(f_s,f_u) <= sum_s pi_s^2 Delta(f_s)`. In addition, the mixed spectral B-energy is exactly `sum_s pi_s(1-pi_s) I(f_s) <= Delta(F)`. The full ledger is `Delta(F)=sum_s pi_s Delta(f_s)+delta_A`, where `delta_A=I_A(F)-Var_A(E_B F)>=0`.

These budgets overlap and cannot be spent twice. The averaged slice deficit gives `( |S_B|-1 )P(S)` on every set with nonempty B part; delta_A gives `|S_A|P(S)` on mixed sets and `(|S_A|-1)P(S)` on pure-heavy sets. Their sum is the one conserved root deficit. This accounting is part of the proof, not a promise of an extra reserve.

**Exact first unsupported line:** there is no established arity-independent normalized entropy reference for `Fhat(S_A,S_B)=sum_s chat_s(S_A) fhat_s(S_B)` that pays all mixed coefficients and the pure-heavy high-degree sector by that ledger. The Gram inequality controls energy covariance, not the logarithmic cost of the whole spectral set. Iterating the one-exceptional theorem multiplies constants and does not close arbitrary |A|. The all-two-weight entropy target therefore remains open.

# A041 stage 3: coherent pure-sector payment, mixed reference, binary review

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Target: the B09 lead's all-bias, arity-independent corrected FEI+ for two-weight thresholds F=2*1{w sum_A X+sum_B Y>=t}-1, arbitrary finite A,B, real w>0,t, and independent interior input biases. Corrected entropy E uses natural logarithms, v_i=4q_i(1-q_i), P(S)=Fhat_mu(S)^2, and E=sum_nonempty P(S)ln(v_S/P(S)); Hplus=E/ln2. Delta=I-Var=sum_nonempty(|S|-1)P(S). Proved scoped replacement: coherent averaging costs at most 3 times coefficient variance, independently of the number or probabilities of the physical slices. The extension is vacuous on pure higher-degree sets when all weights are distinct, and is not an arbitrary-weight FEI+ theorem.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

The complete derivation is PURE_SECTOR_PAYMENT.md, equations (1)-(8). For psi_s(a)=a^2 ln(s/a^2), the function psi_s(a)+3a^2 is convex on |a|<=sqrt(s), including zero by continuous derivative extension. Jensen proves

    psi_s(E a)<=E psi_s(a)+3 Var(a).

This acts on coherent amplitudes; there is no slice-label entropy. For the physical restrictions f_x(B)=F(x,B), Parseval on A identifies Var_A(fhat_x(T)) with sum_{U nonempty}P(U,T). Summing over |T|>=2 and applying the symmetric-threshold baseline pays the pure-B entropy by c D_B plus its corresponding mixed variance. Reversing the groups pays pure A as well. The exact nonnegative ledger is

    Delta=D_A+D_B+M,
    D_A=sum_{U nonempty,T}(|U|-1)P(U,T),
    D_B=sum_{U,T nonempty}(|T|-1)P(U,T),
    M=sum_{U nonempty,T nonempty}P(U,T).

Together the two coherent variance costs are at most 6M, so E_pure,ge2<=c(D_A+D_B)+6M<=c Delta. For k equal-weight groups, write r(S) for occupied-group count. The exact replacement ledger is Delta=sum_j D_j+R, R=sum(r(S)-1)P(S). The summed variance overhead is at most 3r(S)P(S)<=6(r(S)-1)P(S) for every cross-group set. Thus the same pure-sector constant holds for all finite k and real weights after orientation/contraction. The universal singleton contribution is at most 4Delta nats.

The tempting next inequality E_mixed<=K M is FALSE. Actual thresholds with weights 2 on m inputs and 1 on m inputs, threshold 3m, and fair biases are conjunction signs. Every nonempty squared coefficient is 4^(1-2m), and E_mixed/M=2(2m-1)ln2 diverges. This is a route obstruction, not a counterexample to FEI+. Its mixed degree deficit still pays the entropy with asymptotic ratio 4ln2. A mixed reference must retain degree rather than price each mixed atom by mass alone.

For M>0 a complete normalized reference is the product of the mixed spectral marginals p_A/M and p_B/M. The exact identity is E_mixed=E_A^m+E_B^m+M ln M-M TC_spec. Here TC_spec measures the dependence of the two portions of a Fourier set conditioned on being mixed; it is not physical conditional-input TC. No erasure estimate is inferred. At M=0 no conditioning/division is performed.

The first unsupported line is precisely an absolute bound on E_mixed by the root Delta, equivalently a sufficient marginal-reference payment retaining the favorable spectral TC cancellation. The squared-energy Gram identity alone does not prove it. Root deficits in these formulas are explicit partitions, not extra reserves. If E_mixed<=K Delta is later proved, the displayed estimates yield Hplus<=(c+4+K)Delta/ln2; the sum of constants makes every reuse explicit. Nothing enlarges the coefficient-one pivotal-credit refutation to a larger multiplier.

The independent A042 review found no unsupported line within its exact binary claim. Direct spectra yield corner ratio 2+2[A(u)+A(v)]/ln2 and parity ratio [A(1-a)+A(1-b)]/ln2, A(z)=z ln(1/z)/(1-z), a=4p(1-p), b=4q(1-q). ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Polynomial coordinates remove all apparent boundary singularities

For S define w_S=prod_{i in S}4p_i(1-p_i) and

    c_S(p)=2^(-|S|) E_{p outside S}[ Delta_S f ],

where Delta_i f=f(x_i=1)-f(x_i=0), and c_empty=E f. Integration of each selected coordinate gives fhat_p(S)=sqrt(w_S)c_S(p). The polynomial c_S is independent of p_i for i in S; |c_S|<=1 because |Delta_S f|<=2^|S|. Thus

    P(S)=w_S c_S^2,
    E(f)=sum_{S nonempty} w_S c_S^2 ln(1/c_S^2),
    D(f)=sum_{|S|>=2} (|S|-1) w_S c_S^2.                 (1)

Use x ln(1/x)=0 at x=0. Formula (1) extends continuously to the closed bias cube. Any S intersecting a deterministic coordinate has w_S=0, while coefficients on the remaining coordinates are those of the actual restricted Boolean table. This proves path-independent contraction and transfers any fixed-arity inequality E<=C_nat D on the entire interior to its boundary. It does not bound the ratio at a boundary point with D=0 merely by continuity.

More precisely, along a fixed real-analytic one-parameter path into an endpoint, write a nonzero atom as w_S(t)=W_S t^r(1+O(t)), c_S(t)=b_S t^s(1+O(t)), W_S>0, b_S nonzero, r,s nonnegative integers. Then

    P(S)=W_S b_S^2 t^(r+2s)(1+O(t)),
    E_S=W_S b_S^2 t^(r+2s)[2s ln(1/t)-ln(b_S^2)]
         +O(t^(r+2s+1)(1+ln(1/t))).                    (2)

The same P(S) multiplied by |S|-1 supplies its deficit term. When r=0 the O(t) formulation still follows from analyticity. Identically zero atoms contribute zero. For c_S identically of magnitude one, E_S is identically zero. All logarithms in (2) arise from a vanishing normalized c_S, not from the boundary factor w_S. Summing this finite list gives an algorithm for symbolic leading orders along any such path. Formula (2) is fixed-table/path asymptotics, not an arity-uniform remainder or an assertion for nonanalytic paths. Arbitrary paths still have the continuity statement (1).

### 2. One rare coordinate: exact polynomial deficit and entropy expansion

Fix all remaining biases strictly inside (0,1). Let the rare coordinate X have P(X=1)=e, f(0,y)=g(y), f(1,y)=h(y). Use a_T=ghat(T), b_T=hhat(T)-ghat(T), and w_T for the remaining-coordinate normalization. Then exactly

    fhat(T)=a_T+e b_T,
    fhat(T union {X})=sqrt(e(1-e)) b_T.                (3)

Consequently, with D0=D(g),

    D(f)=D0+e L-e^2 Q,
    L=2 sum_{T nonempty} (|T|-1)a_T b_T
        +sum_T |T|b_T^2,
    Q=sum_{T nonempty} b_T^2.                        (4)

The reduction of the quadratic coefficient to -Q includes the empty packet correctly: its coefficient in both degree sums is zero. This identity holds for every e in [0,1]. It also follows without radicals by replacing a_T b_T with w_T c_g(T)(c_h(T)-c_g(T)).

The entropy identity is

    E(f)=sum_{T nonempty} (a_T+e b_T)^2
              ln[w_T/(a_T+e b_T)^2]
         +e(1-e) sum_T b_T^2 ln[4w_T/b_T^2].          (5)

Zero b terms are omitted. The bound |b_T|<=2sqrt(w_T) ensures every log in the second sum is nonnegative. The factor 4e(1-e) in w_{T union X} cancels exactly with the factor e(1-e) in P; the empty T in this second sum must not be discarded.

Define

    K=2 sum_{T nonempty,a_T!=0} a_T b_T
                      (ln[w_T/a_T^2]-1)
          +sum_T b_T^2 ln[4w_T/b_T^2],
    Z=sum_{T nonempty,a_T=0} b_T^2.

For each fixed table and remaining bias vector, Taylor expansion of u^2 ln(w/u^2) at u=a_T!=0, and exact evaluation at u=e b_T for a_T=0, give

    E(f)=E(g)+e K+2 e^2 Z ln(1/e)+O(e^2).            (6)

No first-order e ln(1/e) term occurs. Constants in O(e^2) depend on the fixed spectra and biases and are not uniform when a nonzero a_T approaches zero. For p_X tending to one, exchange g and h and set e=1-p_X. Equations (3)--(6) give the requested zero/one endpoint expansion.

### 3. Zero deficit and unit-atom endpoints

At an interior bias, D=0 means all degree>=2 Fourier coefficients vanish. Hence f is affine in the bits. In the unique multilinear expansion of f^2=1, the coefficient of x_i x_j is twice the product of the two affine slopes. At most one slope is nonzero. A nonconstant affine Boolean function on two values is a signed literal. Thus D=0 iff f is constant or a signed literal; both have E=0 by (1). This proof uses the full truth table, made available by interior probabilities.

If D0=0 and L=0 in (4), nonnegativity of D(f) for e>0 forces Q=0. It follows that D(f)=0 for all e and E(f)=0. There is no permissible division by that zero polynomial. If D0=0 and L>0, (6) gives the finite limit

    lim_{e->0} Hplus(f)/D(f)=K/[(ln 2)L].             (7)

K>=0 follows from E(g)=0, E(f)>=0 and (6). The limit is conditional on this particular fixed-table slice; (7) does not supply a uniform bound over growing arity or varying remaining biases. If D0>0 the ordinary continuous ratio tends to Hplus(g)/D(g). These exhaust the possibilities, since D0=0 and L<0 contradicts (4).

For a unit spectral atom P(U)=1 on the contracted interior cube, Parseval implies f=+/-phi_U pointwise. Since |f|=1 and the coordinates have full independent support, varying any one coordinate in U shows that its two phi_i magnitudes must agree; this forces p_i=1/2. Hence f is a signed parity on fair coordinates U (irrelevant coordinates may have any bias). E=0 and D=|U|-1 for U nonempty. U empty gives a constant and D=0. In particular a unit singleton atom is a fair dictator; a higher-degree unit atom is not a zero-deficit endpoint. Its neighboring slice obeys (4)--(6) with D0=|U|-1. At a deterministic endpoint the assertion is made only after contraction; the unreduced basis is undefined there.

### 4. All three weighted-threshold guards and their order of limits

Write r=q^m, a=q0, z=1-a, A={X0=1} union {X0=0, all light bits=1}, f=2 1_A-1, alpha=a+zr. The source's mixture parameter is eta=P(X0=0 | A), giving a=(1-eta)r/[eta+(1-eta)r]. The exact spectrum is

    P(empty)=(2alpha-1)^2,
    P({0})=4az(1-r)^2,
    P(T)=4z^2 r^2 [(1-q)/q]^k              (T light, k>=1),
    P({0} union T)=4az r^2 [(1-q)/q]^k     (T light, k>=1).

There are binom(m,k) light subsets of size k. This is an actual Boolean weighted threshold with weights m,1,...,1. Its influences are I0=4az(1-r), sum_light Ij=4mzr(1-q), and V=4alpha(1-alpha).

For the sign conjunction g of the light variables, binomial summation gives exactly

    Dg=4r[m(1-q)-1+r],
    Hplus(g)=2Dg+8mr(q-r) log2(1/q).                  (8)

The outer two-bit OR on probabilities a,r has

    Do=4ar z(1-r),
    Hplus_o=8az(1-r)^2 log2[1/(1-r)]
             +8r(1-r)z^2 log2(1/z)+8ar z(1-r).

Direct coefficient summation, or exact disjoint composition, gives

    D(f)=Do+z Dg,    Hplus(f)=Hplus_o+z Hplus(g).     (9)

Using -ln(1-u)<=u/(1-u) proves Hplus_o<=(2+4/ln2)Do. Thus (8)--(9) keep all charges without estimating raw entropy or dividing a deterministic child variance.

Growing arity regime: m>=2, q=m^(-2), eta=1/m, d=1+(m-1)r. Then a=(m-1)r/d, alpha=mr/d, and I/(4alpha)=2-1/m-q-a. The exact conditional law has

    TC(Q)=m h(1/m+(1-1/m)q)-(m-1)h(q)              (nats).

The estimate h(u)=u ln(1/u)+u+O(u^2), uniformly for 0<u<=1/2, gives TC(Q)=ln m+1+O((ln m)/m). Consequently

    alpha TC(Q)/I=(ln m+1)/8+O((ln m)/m).            (10)

For the coefficient-one pivotal credit, rho0=1-r, rho_light=z q^(m-1), and both logarithm arguments exceed one for m>=2. They are (1-r)/[alpha(1-alpha)] and m/(1-alpha). Set Lalpha=ln(1/alpha)=2m ln m-ln m+ln d. The residual R divided by 4alpha is exactly

    (1-alpha)[Lalpha-ln4-ln(1-alpha)]
       -(1-1/m-a)[Lalpha+ln(1-r)-ln(1-alpha)]
       -(1-q)[ln m-ln(1-alpha)].                    (11)

Since r=m^(-2m), all r Lalpha, a Lalpha and alpha Lalpha terms vanish faster than (ln m)/m. Equation (11) is ln m-ln4+O((ln m)/m). Dividing by I/(4alpha) yields

    R/I=(1/2)ln m-ln2+O((ln m)/m).                  (12)

This refutes the credit multiplier one, and changing the fixed factor inside the logarithm contributes only O(I). It makes no claim that all larger multipliers fail.

Meanwhile Do/Dg=O(r), and the extra term in (8), divided by Dg, is O((ln m)/m^2). Equations (8)--(9) give the explicit dimension-varying bound

    Hplus(f)/D(f)=2+O((ln m)/m^2)+O(r) ->2.          (13)

The constants here can be absolute for m>=2, since m(1-q)-1+r>=m/4 and a<=mr. This establishes the growing-arity limit separately rather than misusing the fixed-m series (6).

Third guard, a different limit: fix m>=1 and eta=1/2, then let q->0, with a=r/(1+r). Q tends to the equal mixture of the complementary deterministic vectors (1,0,...,0) and (0,1,...,1). For every distinct pair j,k, I_Q(Xj;Xk)->ln2 by finite-law entropy continuity. Also I0/alpha=2(1-r)/(1+r)->2, and each Ij/alpha=2(1-q)->2. Therefore for every coordinate

    alpha sum_{k!=j} I_Q(Xj;Xk)/Ij -> m ln2/2.      (14)

First take q->0 with fixed m; only then let m grow. No uniform-in-m small-q remainder is asserted. This concerns conditional physical input TC, not spectral erasure. For m>=2, (8)--(9) also give Hplus/D->2 in this fixed-m regime; m=1 is exceptional and has limit 2+4/ln2, as follows directly from the two-bit OR formula. Recording this exception prevents a spurious all-m assertion.

The first unsupported step toward the global target would be inferring an arity-uniform weighted-threshold FEI+ constant from the fixed-table slice coefficient K/L or from these particular guards. No such inference is made.

## executed validation with domain, arithmetic, commands and outputs

Frozen before computation: out/FROZEN_SPEC.json. Executed `python -X utf8 code/check_endpoints.py` from this run (or with its full relative path from the agent root). The standard-library Fraction checker exhausts every sign truth table on n=1,2,3, each distinguished coordinate, remaining rational biases alternating 1/3 and 2/5, and rare bias 1/7 or 6/7: 1,608 fixtures without symmetry reduction. It checks mixed-difference spectra, normalization, (3)--(4), coefficient bounds, zero-deficit classification, and all fair-input unit atoms. It separately compares direct Boolean spectra to the compressed weighted formula for m=1..5, and certifies six weighted finite ratios for m=2,3,4,8,16,32 using rational log enclosures. Result: 24,095 assertions passed, 80 zero-deficit slice fixtures and 28 fair-input unit atoms, measured computation 2.792 seconds. Estimated memory below 80 MB; no heavy search or memok threshold was approached.

For log x, range-reduce x=2^k u with 1<=u<2 and use z=(u-1)/(u+1). The first N=28 terms of 2 sum z^(2h+1)/(2h+1) are a lower bound; the omitted positive tail is at most 2z^(2N+1)/[(2N+1)(1-z^2)]. Negative k uses swapped ln2 endpoints. Signed scaling swaps endpoints, division considers all four rational corner quotients, and outputs are rounded outward to rational multiples of 10^(-10). These enclosures are finite certificates, while the proofs above establish all-parameter limits. CHECK_OUTPUT.json stores the exact outward rational bounds.

# A042 stage 2: exact binary prime endpoint constants

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For every essential binary Boolean sign gate f, certify the exact supremum of Hplus_mu(f)/(I_mu(f)-Var_mu(f)) over every independent interior Bernoulli law (p,q) in (0,1)^2, including biases approaching deterministic endpoints at arbitrary independent rates. Hplus is corrected spectral entropy in bits. I is spectral influence, not unweighted pivotal probability. The denominator D is strictly positive for every essential binary gate at every interior law. At deterministic boundary laws, contract inputs and never divide the resulting zero deficit.

Result: the eight gates with one exceptional truth-table value have supremum C_corner=2+4/ln2; XOR and XNOR have supremum C_xor=2/ln2. Each supremum is unattained in the interior and is approached by explicitly specified Boolean endpoint paths. The sharp constant for all essential binary primes is C_binary=2+4/ln2, in (7.770780163555,7.770780163556). This closes the binary base case, not the all-arity prime inequality or full FEI.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Complete binary classification and invariances

A binary sign table has either zero/four positive entries (constant), two positive entries on one row or column (signed unary literal), two positive entries on a diagonal (XOR/XNOR), or one/three positive entries (one exceptional corner). Thus exactly ten binary functions depend essentially on both inputs: eight corner gates and two parity gates. The six others have D=Hplus=0 for every product law.

Output negation preserves the squared Fourier spectrum, Hplus, I, Var and D. Input reflection Xi -> 1-Xi, accompanied by p_i -> 1-p_i, negates the selected centered character and preserves its variance 4p_i(1-p_i). It therefore preserves each squared mass and Hplus/D. An input reflection is a bijection of the full interior bias domain. These facts reduce the eight corner gates to a conjunction sign, without imposing p=q or any coupling of their rates. Arbitrary biased character multiplication is not used.

Define the scalar function

    A(x)=x ln(1/x)/(1-x),   0<x<1;
    A(0)=0, A(1)=1.                                      (1)

The endpoint values follow from x ln(1/x)->0 and ln(1/x)/(1-x)->1 as x->1. The logarithm inequality ln y<=y-1, strictly for y!=1, gives A(x)<1. Positivity gives A(x)>0. Differentiating yields

    A'(x)=(-ln x-1+x)/(1-x)^2>0,                         (2)

using -ln x>1-x. Hence A is a strictly increasing continuous bijection of [0,1] onto [0,1]. These scalar inequalities certify the continuum, not a finite bias grid.

### 2. The eight corner gates: exact ratio and sharp supremum

Let (b1,b2) be the exceptional corner and let u=P(X1=b1), v=P(X2=b2). Output negation makes the table f=2 1_{X1=b1,X2=b2}-1. Reflections reduce the computation to u=p, v=q. Put V_u=4u(1-u), V_v=4v(1-v). Direct integration gives

    P(empty)=(2uv-1)^2,
    P({1})=V_u v^2,
    P({2})=V_v u^2,
    P({1,2})=V_u V_v/4.

Only the pair contributes to spectral deficit, so

    D=4u(1-u)v(1-v)>0.

The corrected logarithms of singleton packets are log2(1/v^2) and log2(1/u^2), and the pair logarithm is log2 4. Therefore

    Hplus/D = 2+(2/ln2)[A(u)+A(v)].                    (3)

Equations (1)--(3) prove

    2 < Hplus/D < 2+4/ln2

for every interior law, with infimum 2 and supremum 2+4/ln2. Since A(u),A(v) are each strictly increasing, the ratio approaches its supremum if and only if u->1 and v->1. In the original coordinates this means concentration of the product input law at the exceptional corner. The gate itself remains the same essential Boolean truth table throughout the path, even though its contracted limit is constant.

The full boundary ratio map, interpreted as a limit of interior ratios rather than a value at D=0, is explicit: u,v->0 gives 2; u->1,v->0 or vice versa gives 2+2/ln2; u,v->1 gives 2+4/ln2. An edge limit is obtained by replacing only the endpoint A value in (3). This holds along arbitrary independent rates, including nonanalytic paths, because (3) is exact and A has one-variable continuous endpoint extensions.

There is also an explicit uniform two-parameter error certificate. For 0<e<1,

    A(1-e)=1-sum_{k>=1} e^k/[k(k+1)].                (4)

This follows by multiplying the convergent series -ln(1-e)=sum e^j/j by (1-e)/e. The positive remainder after its linear term obeys

    0 <= 1-A(1-e)-e/2 <= e^2/[6(1-e)].                (5)

For u=1-e, v=1-d and arbitrary 0<e,d<=1/2, (3)--(5) give

    C_corner-Hplus/D=(e+d)/ln2+R,
    0<=R<=(e^2+d^2)/(3(ln2)) * 2.                    (6)

The bound follows separately in each coordinate; no relation between e and d, no order of limits and no hidden coefficient lower bound is required. A sharper all-interior remainder is (1/(3 ln2))[e^2/(1-e)+d^2/(1-d)]. Near the opposite endpoint, A(e)=e ln(1/e)+e^2 ln(1/e)+R0 with 0<=R0=e^3 ln(1/e)/(1-e), which exposes the remaining higher-order normalized-coefficient logarithms directly.

### 3. XOR/XNOR: exact ratio, unit atom and sharp supremum

Output negation reduces parity to f=(2X1-1)(2X2-1). Set a=4p(1-p), b=4q(1-q), and m_p=2p-1, m_q=2q-1. Independence gives

    P(empty)=m_p^2 m_q^2,
    P({1})=a m_q^2,
    P({2})=b m_p^2,
    P({1,2})=ab.

Thus D=ab>0. The pair has zero corrected entropy, since its mass equals its normalization. The singleton logarithms give

    Hplus/D=(1/ln2)[A(1-a)+A(1-b)].                  (7)

At a=1 or b=1, define the corresponding term by A(0)=0. This agrees with omitting a zero singleton. Equations (1)--(2) imply 0<=Hplus/D<2/ln2 and show that equality at zero occurs exactly at p=q=1/2, where the pair is a unit spectral atom and D=1. A unit pair is consequently not a zero-deficit case.

The supremum 2/ln2 is approached if and only if p and q each approach some deterministic endpoint. Every one of the four product corners works; their two rates are unrestricted. At an edge with one input fixed fair, the largest ratio limit is 1/ln2. Formula (7) describes every other edge limit. With a,b<=1/2, the uniform error estimate is

    C_xor-Hplus/D=(a+b)/(2 ln2)+R,
    0<=R<=(a^2+b^2)/(3 ln2).                          (8)

This follows from (5). If p=e approaches zero or p=1-e approaches one, a=4e(1-e), so (8) is also an explicit unequal-rate corner certificate in the physical probabilities.

For either class, contracting deterministic inputs produces a constant or unary literal with Hplus=D=0. Assigning the nonzero limiting ratio as an actual ratio of that contracted function would be invalid. The new proof resolves the simultaneous-bias issue for arity two without claiming that stage-one fixed-bias Taylor remainders are uniform for arbitrary gates.

### 4. Focused independent reconstruction of the queued A043 interface

For a proper essential block B with 2<=|B|<=n-1, form the rows f(a,.) indexed by a in {0,1}^B. Genuine one-bit disjoint substitution f=G(h(x_B),x_Bc) implies at most two rows. Essentiality excludes a single row. Conversely two distinct rows define h as the row label and G as the row lookup. Each factor is essential because an irrelevant factor input would be irrelevant in f; the new outer wire is relevant because the rows differ. The split has arities |B| and n-|B|+1, both below n, so recursion terminates and uses each physical leaf once. This reconstructs the analytic cofactor criterion, but makes no claim that the saved n<=4 census was checked.

For a disjoint composition F=G(f1,...,fk) under independent interior physical laws, let eta_i=P(fi=1), V_i=4eta_i(1-eta_i)>0. The centered output function of fi is (fi-E fi)/sqrt(V_i), and its nonempty coefficients are fihat(T)/sqrt(V_i). Disjointness and uniqueness of each block intersection imply

    Fhat(union_{i in U} T_i)
       = Ghat_eta(U) product_{i in U}[fihat(T_i)/sqrt(V_i)]

for nonempty T_i. Squaring and summing identifies each occupied-child law as P_i(T)/V_i. Splitting the corrected logarithm into its outer factor and its child factors gives

    Hplus(F)=Hplus_eta(G)+sum_i alpha_i Hplus(fi),
    D(F)=D_eta(G)+sum_i alpha_i D(fi),
    alpha_i=I_i^eta(G)/V_i>=0.                        (9)

The influence identity is I(F)=sum alpha_i I(fi), and Var(F)=Var_eta(G). This proves the deficit equation in (9). Constant children are frozen and deleted before division by V_i. Under product physical inputs, disjoint child outputs are independent, so the product law eta is the actual induced law. It can be nonuniform even from uniform physical inputs.

This independently reconstructs A043's analytic composition/localization interface and confirms it survives unequal biases and deterministic contraction. The exact census portion of its bundled claim remains outside this stage's review. No substantive repair was supplied to A043; no full bundled acceptance is asserted.

Combining (9) with the sharp binary inequality gives corrected Hplus<=C_binary D for every read-once formula of binary Boolean gates and literals, uniformly in depth and physical biases. Constants are contracted. On uniform physical inputs, H=Hplus+h0, with h0=-t log2 t and t=(E f)^2; -t ln t<=1-t=Var implies h0<=Var/ln2. Hence H<=C_binary I. The corrected constant is sharp already for one binary gate. This is a restricted closure and no claim of a new class theorem in the literature; it is the concrete base case needed by B09's prime localization.

The first unsupported universal inference would be 'all prime arities have the same bound because binary primes do.' The cofactor decomposition leaves ternary majority and unbounded-arity primes, and supplies no inequality for them. None of C018/C019/C020/C024's unpaid universal entropy terms is discharged by binary closure alone.

# A042 stage 3: sharp product-biased majority-three and mixed-rate boundaries

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For the essential ternary prime f(x)=2*1{x1+x2+x3>=2}-1, and all independent interior Bernoulli probabilities p1,p2,p3, determine the corrected entropy/deficit supremum and classify simultaneous deterministic endpoints without coupling their rates. Spectral influence is I=sum |S|P(S), deficit D=I-Var=sum_{|S|>=2}(|S|-1)P(S), natural corrected entropy is E=sum_nonempty P(S)ln(w_S/P(S)), w_S=product_{i in S}4pi(1-pi), and Hplus=E/ln2 is in bits. Contract deterministic coordinates before forming an orthonormal basis or dividing by a zero deficit.

Proved for every interior bias vector:

    E <= (4+ln4)D-(ln4-1)P({1,2,3}).                 (T)

The triple mass is positive and ln4>1, so Hplus/D is strictly less than C*=2+4/ln2. Its exact supremum is C*, approached by the fixed prime along A043's path (1-t^3,t,t). The result also holds under input reflections, permutations and output negation with the corresponding reflected biases. It is a complete all-bias theorem for this prime orbit, not an all-prime or all-weighted-threshold theorem. The new proof is a narrow completion of the endpoint packet; B09's broad pending-review cap is respected.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

At stage start read BOARD.md, snapshot 2026-10-13 22:05 UTC; own STATUS.md and LEARNED.md; and B09 STREAM_NOTES.md v2, still dated 2026-10-11 23:33 UTC. The lead's assigned binary target was completed in stage 2. The updated board reports A041 and A045 independently accepted that binary theorem and A043 identified a higher-prime majority endpoint. Following productive continuation/consuming a new dependency, this stage takes A043's explicit prime rather than repeating binary or one-bit-tax work.

Read A043's stage-2 REPORT.md and MAJORITY_BOUNDARY.md in runs/20261012T012726Z-account05-A043-prime-gate-decomposition, including its exact majority coefficients, essential-prime test, source path and stated gap. Reconstruct that boundary proof independently below. Its finite n<=4 grid certificate is not replayed or independently accepted here. Read A041's stage-3 REPORT.md in runs/20261013T215146Z-account05-A041-weighted-threshold-fei, including its new pure-sector/mixed-reference interface and full binary review; and A045's stage-2 CLAIMS.json at runs/20261013T212421Z-account05-A045-biased-realizability-referee. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Actual Boolean spectrum and prime test

For each two-coordinate block of majority, block Hamming weights zero, one and two give distinct cofactor rows (-1,-1), (-1,+1), (+1,+1). Hence no proper two-coordinate block factors through a Boolean wire: this is a genuine essential ternary prime under A043's disjoint substitution convention.

Put vi=4pi(1-pi) and mi=1-2pi. Write c_S=fhat(S)/sqrt(w_S). The sign polynomial is

    f=2x1x2+2x1x3+2x2x3-4x1x2x3-1.

Integrating centered differences yields

    c_i=pj+pk-2pjpk,    c_ij=(1-2pk)/2,    c_123=-1/2,  (1)

where {i,j,k}={1,2,3}; c_empty=E f. Consequently P(S)=w_S c_S^2. Let

    B=sum_{|S|=2}P(S),   T=P(123)=v1v2v3/4.

Then D=B+2T>0. The notation B here is just pair mass within this proof; it is not the campaign's aggregate erasure entropy.

### 2. Degree-resolved singleton payment preserves the needed triple credit

For a monotone sign table, ci is its pivotal indicator's mean and lies in [0,1]. Conditioning one bit gives fhat(U union {i})=sqrt(vi)*dihat(U), where di is the {0,1}-valued pivotal derivative. Parseval and Var(di)=ci(1-ci) therefore imply, for this three-input table,

    sum_i vi ci(1-ci)=sum_{|S|>=2}|S|P(S)=2B+3T.      (2)

For 0<=c<=1, ln(1/c)<=1/c-1 gives

    c^2 ln(1/c^2)<=2c(1-c),                          (3)

with the zero endpoint interpreted by continuity. Thus the singleton natural entropy obeys

    E_single<=2(2B+3T)=4D-2T.                        (4)

Replacing (4) merely by 4D would discard exactly the credit needed below. This is a degree-resolved version of the general monotone singleton estimate, not an invocation of a separate tax payment.

### 3. Exact pair/triple entropy and the sharp global upper bound

For a pair {i,j}, put z=mk^2. Equation (1) gives

    E_ij=(vi vj/4) z ln(4/z)
         =ln4*P(ij)+(vi vj/4) z ln(1/z).

The elementary inequality -z ln z<=1-z, 0<=z<=1, and 1-mk^2=vk give

    E_ij<=ln4*P(ij)+vi vj vk/4=ln4*P(ij)+T.          (5)

The triple contribution is exactly ln4*T. Summing the three pair bounds and this triple term yields

    E_ge2<=ln4*B+(3+ln4)T
          =ln4*D+(3-ln4)T.                           (6)

Combining (4) and (6) proves (T):

    E<= (4+ln4)D+(1-ln4)T.

There is no reused deficit reserve: (2) counts pairs twice and the triple three times, and (6) spends exactly three triple copies from the three pair normalizations. The residual coefficient is favorable since ln4>1 (for example integrate 1/x>1/2 on (1,2) to obtain ln2>1/2). Every step remains valid at zero pair coefficients by continuity.

The nontrivial identity behind this closure is the shared triple mass in (5). For higher arity, different pairs do not all reduce to one affine missing-coordinate amplitude and one common triple mass; the same summation does not establish an all-arity theorem.

### 4. Full-corner asymptotics at independent rates

Let rare deviations e,u,v>0 tend independently to zero, set delta=max(e,u,v)<=1/8 and S=eu+ev+uv. Both a unanimous vertex and a mixed vertex have exactly

    D=4 sum_{cyc} e u(1-e)(1-u)(1-2v)^2
           +32euv(1-e)(1-u)(1-v).                    (7)

In particular

    |D-4S|<=35 delta S,   D>=S.                      (8)

To check (8), each pair's product factor differs from one by at most e+u+4v<=6delta. Its total error is at most 24delta S. Also T<=16euv<=16delta S/3. The lower bound follows from the pairs alone: D>=4(1-delta)^2(1-2delta)^2 S>=S for delta<=1/8.

At a unanimous zero vertex p=(e,u,v), or the unanimous one vertex by complement, all singleton normalized coefficients are sums of two rare deviations minus twice their product. Thus

    Hplus/D=2+O(delta ln(1/delta)).                    (9)

At a mixed vertex with unique typical one, use p=(1-e,u,v). Here

    c1=u+v-2uv,
    c2=1-(e+v-2ev),   c3=1-(e+u-2eu).

Writing h(c)=c^2 ln(1/c^2), h(1-a)=2a+O(a^2), gives the singleton leading term

    E_single=8e(u+v)+16uv+O(delta S ln(1/delta)).

The higher-degree leading term from (5)--(6) is 4ln4*S+O(delta S). Therefore

    Hplus/D = 2+(2/ln2)(1+lambda)+O(delta ln(1/delta)),
    lambda=uv/[e(u+v)+uv] in (0,1).                  (10)

This includes all six mixed vertices by permutation and simultaneous input/output complement. It does not assume powers of one common parameter or a fixed relative rate. If lambda oscillates, the ratio need not have a limit; (10) still controls every accumulation value.

For completeness, a concrete error certificate valid for every e,u,v<=1/8 is

    absolute error in either (9) or (10)
          <=400 delta ln(1/delta)                    (bits). (11)

Here is an explicit bookkeeping proof. Let t be a sum of two rare deviations, t<=2delta<=1/4. The function t^2 ln(1/t^2) is increasing there. The nonpivotal mixed singleton contribution divided by S is at most 16delta ln(1/delta); the sum of the unanimous singletons divided by S is at most 32delta ln(1/delta). For c=1-a>=3/4, |h''(c)|<=6, so |h(1-a)-2a|<=3a^2. Combining the factors (1-u) and a=e+v-2ev bounds each pivotal-singleton error by 48delta*u(e+v); summing gives at most 96delta S. From (5), the higher-degree entropy differs from 4ln4*S by at most 64delta S, using ln4<3/2, |pair mass-4S|<=24delta S, and T<=16delta S/3. Thus the total mixed numerator error is at most 100delta ln(1/delta)S, since ln(1/delta)>=ln8>2. The mixed leading natural ratio is at most ln4+4<11/2. Combining its denominator error (8) gives natural ratio error less than 200delta ln(1/delta), and division by ln2>1/2 proves (11). The unanimous bound is smaller. The scalar constants are elementary: convexity of 1/x bounds its integral on [1,2] strictly by the trapezoid value 3/4, while ln2=2 sum_{j>=0} (1/3)^(2j+1)/(2j+1)>2/3. These give ln4<3/2 and ln8>2. All constants are absolute and independent of the relative rates.

Consequently the unanimous corner limit is 2. At a mixed corner, every possible limiting ratio lies in [2+2/ln2,2+4/ln2]; every point of that interval is achieved by suitable rates. For lambda in (0,1), choose u=v=t and e=((1-lambda)/(2lambda))*t. The endpoint lambda=0 is achieved by e=t,u=v=t^2; lambda=1 by e=t^3,u=v=t. All probabilities stay interior for sufficiently small t. The last path is exactly A043's source path. It proves the sharp lower bound C* for the global inequality (T), independently of finite computations.

### 5. Remaining boundary strata and zero-deficit contractions

If one input becomes deterministic while the other two biases stay interior, the contracted gate is AND2 or OR2. Its deficit is positive, and continuous E,D reduce the ratio to the already certified binary formula. These face limits are not singular 0/0 cases.

For codimension-two edges, hold the remaining bias r in (0,1) fixed, let e,d tend independently to zero, and write A(r)=r ln(1/r)/(1-r). Remainders below are uniform for r in any fixed compact subinterval of (0,1), not uniform as r itself tends to an endpoint.

For two typical zero bits p=(e,d,r), the contracted table is constant and

    D=4r(1-r)(e+d)+O_r((e+d)^2),
    Hplus/D=2+2A(r)/ln2+O_r((e+d)ln(1/(e+d))).        (12)

Two typical one bits give (12) with r replaced by 1-r. For opposite deterministic bits p=(1-e,d,r), the contracted table is the literal in the third coordinate, and

    Hplus/D=2+(2/ln2)[1+theta A(r)+(1-theta)A(1-r)]
                +O_r((e+d)ln(1/(e+d))),
    theta=e/(e+d).                                  (13)

The same deficit expansion holds. To derive these formulas, the pair masses involving the third input have leading sum 4r(1-r)(e+d); the other pair and triple are second order. In (12), the two rare singleton entropy terms total 8r^2(e+d)ln(1/r); the third singleton is O_r((e+d)^2 ln(1/(e+d))). In (13), the rare singleton terms are 8er^2 ln(1/r) and 8d(1-r)^2 ln(1/(1-r)), while the near-saturated third singleton contributes 8r(1-r)(e+d). Dividing gives the displayed terms. Formula (11), rather than a claimed uniform edge remainder, handles r tending simultaneously to its endpoint.

Equations (9)--(13), the binary faces, and (T) cover every deterministic boundary stratum. At an edge the actual contracted E=D=0, even when the interior ratio has a nonzero limit. At the fully fair interior point, the triple has mass 1/4 and the three singletons mass 1/4 each: D=1/2 and Hplus/D=4. Vanishing pairs at that point cause no division by pair coefficients in the proof.

# A043: exact disjoint prime decomposition and its FEI endpoint

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For every finite-dimensional Boolean sign function, remove irrelevant physical coordinates and factor under genuine disjoint one-bit substitution. Define the remaining essential primes exactly, including the convention that constants and unary literals are terminal rather than prime, every essential binary gate is prime, and parity of arity at least three decomposes. Prove termination, inheritance of induced product laws, and the implication of a dimension-independent corrected FEI+ theorem for all primes at all interior product biases. Entropies are in bits; biased influence is spectral influence, not unweighted pivotal probability.

Completed result: the universal corrected product-biased FEI+ endpoint is equivalent, with the same constant, to that endpoint on the precisely defined prime class. Either endpoint with constant C implies ordinary uniform FEI with constant max{C,log2(e)}. This is a proved conditional implication; its antecedent remains OPEN. No proof or disproof of full FEI is claimed. The supporting definitions and complete proofs are in PRIME_GATE_DEFINITION.tex and PRIME_ENDPOINT_IMPLICATION.md.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

Loading entire text is not a claim of reconstructing every theorem in those sources.
